### Page 1

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 11 月 3 日

1 群益投顧 
台玻 (1802 TT ) 3Q26 營收 141.34 億 元 ，QoQ+17.17% 、YoY+31.76% ； 營 業 毛 利 44.67
億 元 ，QoQ+58.74% 、 YoY+257.07% ， 毛 利 率 約 31.60% ， 主 因受惠 於 Low Dk2 、 Low 
CTE 等 高毛利 T-glass 產品 出 貨 比 重 拉 升 ， 且 毛 利 率 較2Q26 提升 8.27% 至 31.60% ，
亦 優 於 原 預 期 之 26.23% ；稅後淨利 27.05 億元， QoQ+113.66% 、 YoY+876.53% ，稅
後 EPS 0.93 元， 均 優於預期 。 鑒於 Low Dk2/Low CTE 出 貨 動 能 持 續 攀 升， 加 上高階
玻纖布 訂 單 能 見 度 已 達 2027 年，且 2028 年 訂 單 洽 談 順 暢。研究部 預估台玻 2027/2028
年稅後 EPS 分別為 4.37 /6.15 元。給予投資建議 Buy ， 目 標 價 81 元。

研 究 員 ： 蔡 季 衡   
A52320@cim.capital.com.tw

台玻 3Q26 毛 利 率 攀 至 31.60% ， 優 於 預 期 ：  
台玻 3Q26 營收 141.34 億元， QoQ+17.17% 、 YoY+31.76% ；營業毛利 44.67
億 元 ，QoQ+58.74% 、 YoY+257.07% ， 毛 利 率 約 31.60% ； 營 業 利 益 33.84 億
元， QoQ+121.90% 、YoY+2,186.48% ；稅 後 淨 利 27.05 億 元，QoQ+113.66% 、
YoY+876.53% ， 稅 後 EPS 0.93 元。  
研 究 部 評 析 ：  
台玻 3Q26 受惠 於 Low Dk2 、Low CTE 等 高 毛 利 T-glass 產品 出 貨 比 重 拉 升，
Low Dk1 占比 持 穩 ， 帶 動 3Q26 營 業 毛 利 QoQ+58.74% 、 YoY+257.07% ，且
毛利率較 2Q26 提升 8.27% 至 31.60% ， 亦 優 於 原 預 期 之 26.23% 。 因 此 3Q26
稅後淨利 27.05 億 元 、 稅 後 EPS 0.93 元， 均 優 於 原 預 期 之 20.47 億 元 、0.70
元。 1~3Q26 台 玻 累 計 營 收 達 364.40 億 元 ， YoY+18.69% ， 毛 利 率 25.38% ，
稅後淨利 45.65 億 元 ， YoY 由 虧 轉 盈 ， 稅 後 EPS 1.57 元。  
台玻 為 因應 供 應 鏈 對 於高 階 玻 纖 布之 強 勁 需 求，擬 增 建 及 冷 修 窯 爐，以 擴 充
產 能 ，並 提 升 市 占 率。11/02/2026 董 事 會 通 過 ，桃 園 廠 、 鹿 港 廠 窯 爐 增 建 及
子 公 司 台 嘉 蚌 埠 玻 璃 纖 維 有 限 公 司 冷 修擴 充 案 ， 預 估 投 入 金 額 約新台幣 16.3
億 元 及 人 民 幣 11.5 億 元 。  
展望 2027 ~2028 年， Low CTE 當前 出 貨 量 已 較 2026 年 初 顯 著 增 加 ， 預 期 營
收 佔 比 將 於 2027 年 陸 續 提 升 ， 並 於 2028 年 伴 隨 近 年 陸 續 擴 建 之 新 產 能 開
出 後 ，追趕 Low Dk2 。 研 究 部預期 2027 年 Low Dk2 營 收 比 重 仍 將 高 於 Low 
Dk1 及 Low CTE 。目 前 台 玻 高 階 玻 纖 布訂 單 能 見 度已達 2027 年，且 2028 年
訂 單 洽 談 順 暢 。 若 供 應 鏈 產 品 需 求無大 規 模 質 變 ， 台玻 2028 年 玻 纖 布 事 業
群 仍 將 持 續 成 長 。研 究 部 預 估 台 玻 4Q26 底 ~2027 年 玻 纖 布 營 收 比 重 有 望 超
越 平 板 玻 璃 和 玻 璃 器 皿 。 預 估 2027 /2028 年稅後 EPS 分別為 4.37 /6.15 元 。  
投 資 建 議 ：  
台玻 3Q26 營收 141.34 億元， QoQ+17.17% 、 YoY+31.76% ；營業毛利 44.67
億 元，QoQ+58.74% 、YoY+257.07% ，毛 利 率 約 31.60% ，主 因受惠 於 Low Dk2 、
Low CTE 等 高 毛 利 T-glass 產品 出 貨 比 重 拉 升 ， 且 毛 利 率 較2Q26 提升 8.27%
至 31.60% ，亦 優 於 原 預 期 之 26.23% ；稅 後 淨 利 27.05 億 元，QoQ+113.66% 、
YoY+876.53% ，稅後 EPS 0.93 元， 均 優 於 預 期 。 鑒 於 Low Dk2/Low CTE 出
貨 動 能 持 續 攀 升，加 上高 階 玻 纖 布 訂 單 能 見 度 已 達 2027 年，且 2028 年訂單
洽 談 順 暢 。研 究 部 預 估 台 玻 2027/2028 年 稅 後 EPS 分別為 4.37 /6.15 元。給
予 投 資 建 議 Buy ， 目 標 價 81 元。

產業速報

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 2

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 服務業 PMI 由 55.4 降至 54.9，連續第 27 個月維持擴張。
商業活動及新訂單雖較前月回落 ， 仍保持穩健成長 ； 就業重返擴張區間 ，
內需與勞動市場仍具韌性 。 惟價格指數續升創高 ， 出口與進口動能轉弱 ，
後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化。 
⚫ 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月增加13.7%。
出口 3,152 億美元、進口4,208 億美元，分別月增1.4%及 4.3%。出口成
長主要受非貨幣黃金、原油及半導體帶動，進口則以原油、黃金及資本
財增加為主 。 累計前8 月貿易逆差 5,570 億美元 ， 較去年同期縮減19.9%，
惟近期進口增幅高於出口，使逆差再度擴大。 
⚫ 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，AI 投資持續帶動經濟與就業動能，勞動市
場仍具韌性；惟去通膨進展有限，核心服務通膨及成本轉嫁壓力仍存。
全體官員支持升息 1 碼，傾向維持限制性政策，並關注 AI 融資、公債
市場波動及網路安全等金融風險。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期美伊緊張情勢再升溫，川普改口不排除選前對伊朗採取軍事行動，
伊朗則持續尋求重啟談判；同時葉門青年運動頻繁襲擊沙國目標，加劇
中東安全風險。若衝突擴大，恐影響荷姆茲與曼德海峽能源運輸，推升
油氣價格並增添全球經濟下行風險。 
⚫ 美國啟動 337 調查，針對 AI 伺服器垂直供電系統（VPD）相關專利侵
權爭議展開審查，鴻海、台達電、廣達等約 20 家業者列名。由於涉及
AI 伺服器關鍵供電技術，若最終認定侵權，相關產品恐面臨輸美限制，
對供應鏈營運帶來影響。 
短評 
⚫ 美國服務業與內需仍具韌性，AI 投資亦支撐經濟動能，惟貿易逆差擴
大、成本壓力升高及通膨黏著，使聯準會仍需維持偏緊縮立場。加上美
伊與中東局勢升溫，能源價格與金融市場波動風險上升，後續景氣不確
定性仍高。

歐 
元 
區 
法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
經濟 ⚫ 德國 8 月工業生產月增率為 2%。 ⚫ 法國 8 月工業生產月增率為-0.3%。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 西班牙政府宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選。 
⚫ 法國 10 年期公債殖利率高出德國 130 個基點以上，主權風險升溫。 
⚫ 歐中談判最終達成 16 項共識，依據歐盟發布聲明，中國將自主減少混
合動力車對歐盟的出口量 5 成以上 ， 雙方同意降低部分產品MFN 稅率 ，
預期 2027 年 3 月雙方將再度舉行貿易會談。 
短評 
⚫ 考量法國財政不確定性較高，短期內德國與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。 
⚫ 法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐相對有限下 ， 景氣復甦力道恐仍
較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現分化的情形短期內仍將
持續。 
日 
本 
薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
經濟 ⚫ 8 月薪資年增 3.8%。 ⚫ 9 月消費者信心下降 0.1 點至 35.4。 
短評 ⚫ 雖然通膨預期回升與物價壓力抑制部分消費信心 ， 但薪資與所得成長仍 持續支撐家庭支出，使消費者信心維持溫和復甦趨勢。 
中 
國 
歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
經濟 ⚫ 中國 9 月外匯存底較 8 月減少 381 億美元至 3.4 兆美元。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 中國財政部宣布動用 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務額度，資金將優
先用於「六網」基礎建設等重點項目，加強穩增長措施。 
短評 
⚫ 歐中談判最終達成 16 項共識，依據歐盟發布聲明，中國將自主減少混
合動力車對歐盟的出口量 5 成以上 ， 雙方同意降低部分產品MFN 稅率 ，
預期 2027 年 3 月雙方將再度舉行貿易會談。

3

東 
(南) 
亞 
世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息 
經濟 
⚫ 世銀上調東亞及太平洋地區 2026 年 GDP 預估至 4.5%；印度 2026 財年
GDP 預估上調至 7.1%。 
⚫ 越南 2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率達 9.95%。 
重要 
事件 ⚫ 印度央行升息一碼至 5.5%，以應對通膨及匯率壓力。 
短評 
⚫ 世銀認為人工智慧 （AI） 順風仍足以抵銷能源風險，但警告亞洲地區經
濟高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容易受到全球科技支出逆轉的
影響，此外，能源震撼延續到明年，補貼政策恐難以為繼。 
台 
灣 
出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
經濟 ⚫ 9 月消費者物價指數年增 2.73%。 ⚫ 9 月出口額為 872.2 億美元。 
重要
事件 
⚫ 美國國際貿易委員會 （USITC)以 337 條款 ， 對特定垂直供電系統及相關
產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達、鴻海及旗下相關企業等遭
列名，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。 
短評 
⚫ 9 月出口續創佳績，反映 AI 相關需求及科技產品拉貨動能強勁，外需
表現維持熱絡；9 月 CPI 創近兩年半新高，主要受天候因素及能源價格
影響，但主計總處預期 10 月將回落。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.12 
一、 美國—美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI) 為 54.9，較上月
55.4 下降 0.5 個百分點，已連續第 27 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近 12
個月平均值 54.1，服務業景氣仍持續穩健擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由 61.7
降至 56.5，新訂單指數由 60.9 降至 59.8，雖較前月回落，但仍維持高檔擴張；就
業指數則由 47.8 回升至 50.1，三個月來首度重返擴張區間。價格指數由 72.6 升
至 74.0，創 2022 年 7 月以來新高。供應商交貨指數由 51.3 升至 53.2，交貨時間
再度延長；庫存指數由56.7 升至 57.8，企業持續提前備貨以因應未來成本上升風
險。此外，未完成訂單指數升至 56.6，創 2022 年 7 月以來高點，惟新出口訂單
指數由 56.3 大幅降至 46.9，八個月來首度跌入收縮區間，進口指數亦由 56.3 降
至 52.9，外部需求及採購動能均降溫。整體而言，美國服務業景氣仍維持穩健擴
張，內需與就業展現一定韌性，但燃料價格上漲、關稅政策、供應鏈限制及地緣
政治等因素持續推升企業成本，後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化對景氣的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月修正後928
億美元增加 127 億美元，增幅 13.7%。當月出口 3,152 億美元，較上月增加 45 億
美元(+1.4%)；進口 4,208 億美元，較上月增加 172 億美元(+4.3%)。其中，商品貿
易逆差擴大至 1,366 億美元，服務貿易順差則小幅增至 310 億美元。出口成長主
要受工業用品及原材料出口增加 63 億美元帶動，其中非貨幣黃金、原油及燃油
出口增幅較為顯著，資本財出口亦受半導體、電腦及電腦周邊設備需求增加所支
撐；進口方面，工業用品及原材料進口增加 91 億美元，資本財進口增加 62 億美
元，其中原油、非貨幣黃金、半導體及工業機械進口增幅較大。累計2026 年 1 至
8 月，美國商品及服務貿易逆差為 5,570 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少 1,382 億美
元，縮減 19.9%；同期出口成長 11.8%，進口增加 4.4%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡
情況較去年同期有所改善，惟近期進口增幅明顯高於出口，使貿易逆差再度呈現
擴大趨勢。 
2026 年 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)經濟：若干官員認為美國經濟成
長動能有所增強 ，AI 投資規模與速度持續超乎預期 ， 帶動企業投資與生產力提升 ；

5

企業活動則受到企業獲利維持高檔及部分政策因素支持；(2)就業：多數官員表示
勞動市場近期略有改善，就業成長維持穩健，失業率仍處低檔，整體勞動市場大
致符合充分就業狀態，供需風險大致平衡；(3)通膨：官員普遍認為近期去通膨進
展有限，非住房核心服務通膨仍偏高，人力、能源及部分進口成本轉嫁情形仍值
得關注；部分官員擔憂 AI 投資與相關支出擴張，可能推升需求並增加通膨持續
性風險；(4)貨幣政策：鑑於經濟活動持續擴張、勞動市場具韌性且通膨仍高於目
標，全體官員支持升息 1 碼。許多官員認為維持較具限制性的政策立場有助降低
通膨風險，部分官員則認為未來可能仍需進一步緊縮政策，以確保通膨回到目標
水準；(5)金融風險：官員普遍認為金融條件整體仍支持經濟成長，但部分官員關
切 AI 基礎設施快速擴張所衍生的融資風險，以及美國公債市場波動、網路安全
與其他潛在金融穩定風險。 
近期美伊及中東局勢再度升溫，市場對區域衝突擴大的疑慮升高。美國總統
川普由先前宣稱期中選舉前不會攻擊伊朗 ， 改口表示 「仍有可能」 採取軍事行動 ，
且美軍已就相關軍事選項進行準備，使美伊對抗風險再度攀升；另一方面，伊朗
雖有意透過斡旋重啟談判，但雙方在核計畫與制裁議題上仍存重大分歧。此外，
葉門青年運動持續升高對沙烏地阿拉伯攻擊，利雅德哈立德國王國際機場一週內
多次遇襲，造成多人死傷，凸顯區域安全風險升高。整體而言，若美伊衝突或代
理人戰事進一步升級，恐衝擊荷姆茲海峽及曼德海峽等能源運輸要道，推升國際
油氣價格、加劇通膨壓力，並增添全球金融市場波動及經濟成長下行風險。 
美國國際貿易委員會(USITC)於 10 月 9 日正式啟動 337 條款調查，針對特定
垂直供電系統(Vertical Power Delivery，VPD)、 相關零組件及採用該技術的運算設
備是否涉及專利侵權展開審查。此案由美國電源技術廠商 Vicor Corporation 提出
申訴，主張相關產品侵犯其專利權，並要求發布有限排除令及停止侵權命令，以
限制涉案產品進入美國市場 。 調查對象涵蓋全球約20 家企業 ， 包括台灣的鴻海 、
台達電、廣達及其美國關係企業，另有中國的立訊精密、工業富聯、德國的英飛
凌等國際大廠列名 。 本案聚焦 AI 伺服器及高效能運算領域關鍵的供電架構技術 ，
該技術有助提升晶片供電效率、降低能源損耗，已廣泛應用於資料中心與 AI 運
算設備。隨著全球 AI 基礎建設投資持續擴大，相關專利布局與授權爭議亦日益
升溫。USITC 指出，此次立案僅代表調查程序正式展開，並不表示已認定侵權，
後續將由行政法官進行聽證及審理，預計於 45 天內訂定調查時程。台灣經濟部

則表示，337 調查屬企業間智慧財產權爭議，政府將持續關注案情發展，並於必
要時提供業者相關協助 ； 若最終認定違反337 條款 ， 涉案產品可能面臨輸美限制 ，
對 AI 伺服器供應鏈帶來潛在影響。 
二、歐元區—法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
 德國工業生產在 7 月月減 1.2%後，8 月回彈 2%，主要反映部分汽車業者在 7
月提前進行歲修 ， 並且在8 月陸續復工後 ， 帶動生產及庫存回補需求所致 ； 此外 ，
受到 8 月萊茵河水位降低，沿岸化學工廠物流運輸受阻，致化工產業產出月減
1.2%為主要拖累因素。儘管工業生產如期反彈，惟工業訂單卻大幅下跌 10.6%，
主要是與軍工產業相關的訂單如飛機、船舶、軍用車輛、武器彈藥等訂單減少，
顯示政府前期大額採購效應逐漸消退；除軍工產業外，其餘產業訂單則大致維持
穩定。 
 法國 8 月工業生產月減 0.3%，為連續第二個月下滑，主要係夏季用電高峰過
後電力產出回落，帶動能源部門生產減少所致。另方面，製造業生產月增0.3%，
其中造紙、化工及電子電機設備等產業自前期低點回升，部分抵銷汽車等運輸設
備產出月減 2.2%的拖累。不過，法國 9 月 INSEE 新訂單指標由 8 月的-13.8 點降
至-15.8 點，顯示企業接單偏弱，主要反映出口需求疲軟。此外，相較於德國近期
透過國防及基礎建設支出擴大支撐工業生產，法國財政擴張力道相對有限，使製
造業景氣較依賴外部需求，德、法兩國製造業表現因而呈現逐漸分化的趨勢。 
 展望未來，近期歐元區各國殖利率上升，除反映通膨壓力增強、升息預增加
之外，亦部分反映西班牙宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選、法國財政前景不明朗
等政治因素影響。儘管較高的殖利率有助於促使法國政府推動財政整頓，但考量
相關改革涉及福利及退休制度調整等政治敏感議題，不確定性較高，短期內德國
與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。整體而言，法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐
相對有限下，景氣復甦力道恐仍較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現
分化的情形短期內仍將持續。 
三、日本—薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
 8 月薪資年增 3.8%，其中經常性薪資年增 3.8%、加班費年增 5.2%、實質薪資
年增 1.5%， 主要受惠於春鬥加薪效應延續及通膨壓力較去年趨緩所帶動 ， 有助於
進一步提升民眾購買力，支撐消費與經濟復甦。

9 月消費者信心指數小幅下滑 0.1 點至 35.4，但表現仍優於市場預期。受食品
及商品價格持續上漲影響 ， 民眾對未來一年通膨的預期升溫 ， 預期通膨率高於2%
的家庭比例升至 79.3%，並使生活水準及耐久財購買意願略為轉弱。不過，穩健
的薪資成長及股市上漲帶來的金融收入，持續支撐家庭所得，有助於緩衝物價上
漲對消費的負面影響，顯示日本內需仍具一定韌性。 
四、中國—歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
中國 9 月外匯存底降至 3.40 兆美元，較前月減少 381 億美元，反映美元升
值、政府以外匯購買黃金儲備，加上歐美殖利率上升，造成中國持有的海外債券
資產出現評價損失，皆為外匯存底下滑的主要原因。惟亦不排除中國外匯流入情
況可能有所轉弱，例如出口成長放緩、進口需求增加，或資本外流規模高於預期
等因素。 
展望未來 ， 近期歐中談判達成16 項共識 ， 未來中國混合動力車出口至歐盟將
面臨調整壓力，但歐盟進一步升高貿易保護措施的風險已降溫。另一方面，基於
中國維持大額經常帳順差、出口商結匯需求及年底季節性資金流入支撐，預期人
民幣仍有升值空間，並帶動外匯存底回升。 
五、印度及東南亞—世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息 
(1)世界銀行 2026 年 10 月《東亞與太平洋地區經濟展望報告》指出，受人工智慧
（AI） 相關出口與投資強勁帶動，將2026 年東亞與太平洋地區 （EAP） 經濟成長
率預測上修 0.3 個百分點至 4.5%，2027 年與 2028 年將分別放緩至 4.4%與 4.3%，
其中，越南、馬來西亞、泰國 GDP 大幅上調至 7.4%、5.1%及 2.0%。世銀指出，
AI 相關商品占多數經濟體出口增長的一半以上，其中，馬來西亞、菲律賓、泰國
和越南的 AI 出口貢獻率更超過七成，但扣除 AI 相關產品後，區域貿易成長呈現
疲弱或負增長，警告該地區的經濟韌性高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容
易受到全球科技支出逆轉的影響，建議應將 AI 產品生產優勢轉化為廣泛應用，
以提升生產力，並創造更多優質就業機會。此外，美伊戰爭推升能源價格，各國
政府比其他地區更積極因應 ， 也更仰賴補貼 ， 此將增加財政成本並減少外匯存底 ，
世銀預估中東石油出口要到 2027 年中才會恢復至戰前水準，能源成本上升已對
製造業造成壓力 ， 運輸價格大漲也已打擊消費需求 ， 持續性的補貼可能難以為繼 。

(2)印度:印度央行 10/7 貨幣政策會議宣布升息 1 碼至 5.5%，為近四年來首度升
息，貨幣政策立場從「中性」調整為「有節制的緊縮」。央行總裁指出，目前已
有跡象顯示，通膨預期升高，且價格壓力正逐漸擴散至更廣泛的領域。經濟展望
方面，印度內需、投資及基礎建設支出持續支撐景氣，央行將 2026/27 財年經濟
成長率預測由 6.7%上調至 7.1%，另受中東局勢緊張、國際油價上漲、食品價格
攀升及氣候因素影響，將 2026/27 財年 CPI 通膨率預測上調至 5.2%，高於 4%的
中期目標。另外，世界銀行 10 月最新《南亞經濟展望》報告，上修印度 2026/27
財年經濟成長率 0.4 個百分點至 7.1%，反映內需消費、製造業、服務業及出口表
現優於預期，然而，中東衝突導致能源價格上漲、聖嬰現象衝擊農業生產、全球
金融市場波動及資本流動風險，仍可能影響經濟前景。整體而言，印度經濟成長
動能仍穩健，但外部風險及輸入性通膨壓力增加，未來貨幣政策將以維持物價穩
定為優先，並有進一步升息的可能。 
 (3)越南: 2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率達 9.95%，高於上季修正後的 8.81%，並
創 4 年新高，累計前三季經濟成長率達 9.01%。成長動能主要來自製造業與營建
業擴張、外人直接投資持續流入、以及外貿大幅成長。各產業均穩健成長，工業
及建築業成長 12.50%(占 GDP 總值 51.57%)；服務業成長 9.54%(占 43.29%)；農
業成長 4.21%(占 5.14%)。以支出面觀之，民間消費成長 8.96%，固定投資成長
21.39%，貨物和服務出、進口分別成長23.27%、28.75%。展望未來，越南受惠於
全球企業分散供應鏈、AI 產業需求及政府推動基礎建設與數位轉型 ， 經濟前景仍
偏正向。

六、台灣—出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
9 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.73%，創近兩年半新高，已連續 5 個
月高於 2%警戒線，主要係受豪雨影響，蔬果供應減少帶動價格上漲，加上汽機
車減稅效應淡化、中秋節落點不同及國際油價維持高檔，共同推升物價。除蔬果
外，外食費、房租、電費及交通相關費用皆持續上漲，顯示物價壓力仍在，但主
計總處認為整體漲勢仍屬溫和，預估 10 月 CPI 將略為回落至約 2.5%。 
9 月出口額達 872.2 億美元，年增 60.9%，其中資通產品出口倍增、電子零組
件年增 45.3%。財政部指出，在AI 相關投資持續、部分電子產品供應偏緊、新品
上市與旺季拉貨等因素共同挹注下，出口表現明顯升溫；隨著 AI 應用擴大及年
底採購需求增加，全年出口額有望突破 9,000 億美元。 
美國國際貿易委員會 （USITC）於10 月 9 日依據 《1930 年關稅法》 第337 條
款，對特定垂直供電系統及相關產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達及鴻
海旗下相關企業等遭列名。若 337 條款調查最終認定侵權，相關產品可能遭禁止
輸入美國，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。

2344.TT 華邦電 
 持續看好營運強勁成長

[法說會更新]   
   公司簡介 
華邦電子是利基型記憶體與積體電路解決方案供應商，核心產品包括 DRAM、NOR 
Flash、NAND Flash 以及邏輯 IC 產品，廣泛應用於手持裝置、消費性電子、電腦周
邊 、 車用與工業電子等市場 。2026 年產品組合為 CMS 59%、Flash31%、Logic 10%。    
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
強力買進 205 
【前次投 資建議 】強力 買進，240 
【大盤指數/股價】43308 / 146

➢ 3Q26 報價漲幅強勁，惟位元出貨量下滑 
3Q26 營收 823.3 億元，QoQ+37.58%，YoY+278.18%，成長動能主要
仍來自於記憶體短缺所帶來的報價強勁漲幅，惟公司為因應 LTA 需求而
實行嚴格控貨策略，導致出貨量呈現下滑。獲利表現方面，3Q26 毛利率
約 70.57%，QoQ+4.32ppts，YoY+23.88ppts， 稅後淨利354.22 億元 ，
QoQ+45.67%，YoY+1,103.54%，EPS 為 7.58 元。  
➢ 4Q26 Flash 產品仍具強勁上漲動能，獲利表現可望持續提升 
展望 4Q26，預估 DRAM 產品 ASP 漲幅約逾 10%，NOR 產品漲幅可望
逾 20%。 預估4Q26 營收約 994.8 億元 ，QoQ+20.83%，YoY+273.64%，
毛利率 72.25%， 稅後淨利416 億元 ，QoQ+17.44%，YoY+1,115.55%，
EPS 為 8.81 元。  
➢ 2027 年 DRAM 及 Flash 新產能開出，挹注營收成長動能 
公司目標將高雄廠產能擴增至 24K，隨新產能於 2H26 投片，且新增產
能皆將採用 16nm 製程，逐步取代舊有的 25Snm 與部分 20nm 製程，
位元出貨量預估將會大幅增加。考量記憶體供需吃緊帶動報價漲幅優於
預期 ， 且隨著位元出貨量逐季成長 ， 投研部預估2026 年全年營收約 2799
億元，毛利率 67.89%，稅後淨利 1114.5 億元，EPS 為 24.04 元。展望
2027 年，預期全球市場將面臨更為緊俏且吃緊的供需格局，而受惠於約
5K 的產能擴充，Flash 2027 年的位元出貨量成長率可望突破 40%。
DRAM 方面 ， 高雄廠則預計滿載月產能將達24~25K， 並透過製程轉換 ，
提高 16nm 製程比重，進一步推成位元出貨量。投研部預估 2027 年營
收約 6,008 億 元 ， 毛 利 率71.21% ， 稅 後 淨 利 為2,550 億元，
YoY+128.84%，EPS 為 54.03 元。  
➢ CUBE 及矽電容陸續貢獻營收，中長期營運動能正向 
評價方面，考量公司 3Q26 ASP 漲勢優於先前預期，且 4Q26 Flash 產
品仍具上漲空間，再加上 2027 年位元出貨量可望迎來顯著成長，營收
成長動能強勁之餘，中長期亦有 CUBE 以及矽電容營收貢獻，預期營運
至 2028 年可望維持高成長態勢，故投研部維持強力買進建議，目標價
205 元(2027F PE 4X)。

公司簡介 
華邦電子股份有限公司成立於 1987 年 9 月，公司在中科與南科高雄園區各設有一座
12 吋晶圓廠 。華邦電子是利基型記憶體與積體電路解決方案供應商 ， 同時擁有自有產
品及技術，從產品設計、研發、晶圓製造到品牌行銷完整垂直整合。核心產品包括
DRAM、NOR Flash、NAND Flash 以及邏輯 IC 產品，廣泛應用於手持裝置、消費性
電子 、 電腦周邊 、 車用與工業電子等市場 。2026 年產品組合為 CMS53%、Flash 35%、
Logic 11%。 
圖 1：華邦電產品組合

資料來源：公司、國票投顧預估 
3Q26 報價漲幅強勁，惟位元出貨量下滑 
3Q26 營收 823.3 億元，QoQ+37.58%，YoY+278.18%，營收成長動能主要仍來自
於記憶體短缺所帶來的報價強勁漲幅 ，惟營收仍低於先前預期 ， 主要因公司為因應 LTA
需求而策略性保留部分產能，實行嚴格控貨策略，導致產能利用率雖然滿載，出貨量
呈現下滑。產品報價漲幅方面皆優於預期，DRAM 產品 ASP 約季增 60%，Flash 產
品 ASP 則季增逾 30%；然而在位元出貨量方面，DRAM 出貨量約季減 8%，Flash 產
品則受惠於高容量 SLC/NOR 需求維持強勁，產能利用率滿載，出貨量與 2Q26 大致
持平。獲利表現方面，3Q26 毛利率約 70.57%，QoQ+4.32ppts，YoY+23.88ppts，
稅後淨利 354.22 億元，QoQ+45.67%，YoY+1,103.54%，EPS 為 7.58 元。 
4Q26 Flash 產品仍具強勁上漲動能，獲利表現可望持續提升 
展望 4Q26，雖然面臨消費性電子產品淡季修正，部分客戶在 4Q26 進入庫存調節，
然而 AI 伺服器、車用與 Edge AI 等領域的拉貨動能依然保持強勁。預估 DRAM 產品
ASP 漲幅約逾 10%，NOR 產品則受惠於 AI 伺服器對 512Mb 以上高容量 NOR Flash
的大量需求，短期缺口難以填補，市場報價仍具備繼續上行的空間，預估 ASP 漲幅仍
可望逾 20%。位元出貨量方面，預期 DRAM 可望受惠製程升級以及良率提升，出貨
量下滑幅度可望收斂，Flash 產品則可望在台中廠產能將針對 24 奈米進行小量擴產，
帶動位元出 貨量逐季 提升。預估 4Q26 營收約 994.8 億 元，QoQ+20.83% ，
YoY+273.64%，毛利率 72.25%，QoQ+1.68ppts，YoY+30.38ppts，稅後淨利 416
億元，QoQ+17.44%，YoY+1,115.55%，EPS 為 8.81 元。

2027 年 DRAM 及 Flash 新產能開出，挹注營收成長動能 
目前高雄廠月產能約為 15K，公司目標在年底前將 16nm 機台買足至 16K 的投片規
模，並計畫在年底前將高雄廠產能擴增至 24K，隨著新產能於 2H26 開始投片，且新
增產能皆將採用 16nm 製程，逐步取代舊有的 25Snm 與部分 20nm 製程，位元出貨
量預估將會大幅增加，預估 2H26 僅少量 16nm 產品將貢獻營收，預計 2027 年營收
貢獻將大幅提升。考量記憶體供需吃緊帶動報價漲幅優於預期，且隨著位元出貨量逐
季成長，投研部預估 2026 年全年營收約 2,799 億元，YoY+213%，毛利率 67.89%，
稅後淨利 1,114.5 億元，YoY+2713.11%，EPS 為 24.04 元。 
展望 2027 年，預期全球市場將面臨更為緊俏且吃緊的供需格局，而受惠於約 5K 的
產能擴充，Flash 2027 年的位元出貨量成長率可望突破 40%。此外，由於原廠陸續將
低容量 2D NAND EOL，促使低容量 eMMC 需求轉向 SLC NAND，使 SLC NAND 的
供給在 2027 年維持緊縮狀態。此外，在伺服器端，預估全新的AI 伺服器機櫃，單一
機櫃將採用達 500 顆至 600 顆的 NOR 與 NAND Flash 晶片，尤其拉動 512Mb、
1Gb 及 2Gb 等高容量快閃記憶體的出貨。DRAM 方面，預計 2027 年將增加來自於
Enterprise SSD 的營收貢獻，目前華邦電已經在進行送樣，看好 Enterprise SSD 需
求持續提升之下，可望為華邦電帶來額外 10%的 DRAM 營收貢獻。產能方面，如前
段所述，預計台中廠將額外擴增 5K Flash 產能，並且陸續降低 32nm 等舊製程投片
量，將產能轉移至較為先進的 Flash 製程；高雄廠則預計滿載月產能將達 24~25K，
並透過製程轉換，提高 16nm 製程比重，進一步推成位元出貨量。投研部預估 2027
年營收約 6,008 億元，YoY+114.64%，毛利率 71.21%，YoY+3.3ppts，稅後淨利為
2,550 億元，YoY+128.84%，EPS 為 54.03 元。 
展望後市，產品應用的層面將隨著 Edge AI、On-device AI 以及 Physical AI 的興起
而顯著擴張，尤其在邊緣端設備上，NOR Flash 及 NAND Flash 的容量與數量需求都
將出現爆發性成長；DRAM 則將隨著前述 eSSD 的需求增加而帶動出貨成長。為了因
應 2028 年後更為龐大的市場需求，華邦已經通過 731 億元的資本支出案，正式啟動
下一階段高雄廠 Module B 的擴產計畫。該擴建工程預計 2029 年 1 月開始裝機，預
計於 2H29 小量出貨。Module B 的無塵室面積約為目前高雄廠的兩倍，滿載產能可
望達 50K 至 60K 的月產能，預計將採分階段裝機投產。製程方面，Module B 將採用
16 奈米、14 奈米及 12 奈米製程，並規劃在第二階段引進 EUV。 
此外，針對矽電容佈局方面，公司目前具備 5,400 nF/mm²超高容值規格的技術，具
備極薄化、耐高溫與優異的高頻反應特性。面對 AI 晶片先進封裝空間緊湊及傳統
MLCC 缺貨之痛點，華邦電矽電容可直接嵌入PCB 或 CoWoS 中介層，滿足光模組與
AI 加速晶片對高頻穩定供電的需求。公司採 Foundry 定位，目前產能已被 MLCC 及
AI 供應商包光，並已於高雄廠 Module B 規劃專屬線擴產，預估 2027 年後將成為
CUBE 之外的重要動能。 
CUBE 及矽電容陸續貢獻營收，中長期營運動能正向 
整體而言 ， 隨著華邦電4Gb DDR4/DDR3 出貨占比提升 ， 以及3Q26 陸續出貨的 8Gb 
DDR4 加入，可望改善產品組合，配合報價漲勢提升獲利表現；Flash 方面除了前述
受供給面影響而使報價呈上行趨勢外，預期華邦電將持續維持全球第一大 NOR Flash
供應商的領導地位，並受惠於長期伺服器與 AI 高容量應用的滲透率提升，同時隨車
用 、 消費性電子產品規格升級 ， 對於NOR Flash 或 SLC NAND 的需求亦見同步增長 ，
故公司於 2026 年再度追加 NOR 及 NAND 的產能擴充資本支出，顯見其對於後市正

### Page 4

- 4 -

個股報告 
向看待；最後，在中長期方面，CUBE 目前已進入與客戶合作開發階段，主要針對 AI 
ASIC 及邊緣運算需求，其優勢在於採用3D 堆疊技術，4 層堆疊的頻寬可對標 HBM，
但功耗僅為其 20%，極具競爭力，預計將於 2027 年開始顯著貢獻營收，同時公司新
增矽電容業務，預計將成為公司長期成長的關鍵動能。 
評價方面，考量公司 3Q26 ASP 漲勢優於先前預期，且 4Q26 Flash 產品仍具上漲空
間，再加上 2027 年位元出貨量可望迎來顯著成長，營收成長動能強勁之餘，中長期
亦有 CUBE 以及矽電容營收貢獻，預期營運至 2028 年可望維持高成長態勢，故投研
部維持強力買進建議，目標價 205 元(2027F PE 4X)。

台光電(2383)     買進 報告日期：20261104 
                                                  研究員：陳彥翰

公司基本資料 
前日收盤價(元) 4,395.00  
目前股本(億元) 35.83  
市值(億元) 15,747.30  
每股淨值(元) 205.03  
外資持股比例(%) 42.40  
投信持股比例(%) 11.88  
董監持股比例(%) 8.58  
融資餘額(張) 3,308

個股簡評 
高階銅箔基板產能供不應求，載板可望通過
驗證並成為新成長動能，以上將帶動產品組
合持續大幅優化，推升報價和毛利率，讓今
明兩年營運獲利續創歷史新高 。合庫預估  
2026 / 2027 年獲利分別成長 205% / 121%，
稅後每股盈餘  127.17 / 280.45  元，建議買
進，目標價 5,610 元 (2027 PER 20x)。 
業務營收比重

公司簡介  
台光電子材料股份有限公司成立於 1992 年，
初期主要供應  FR-4 銅箔基板與膠片；並於  
2013 年 ， 成為全球最大無鹵素基板供應商 ， 迄
今仍在市場保持領先地位 。 台光電持續開發出
各種優良的無鹵素新產品，從  Mid. Loss 、 
Low Loss、Very Low Loss、Ultra Low Loss 到 
Extreme Low Loss 等不同等級材料 ， 並符合各
種 高 精 密 先 進 PCB 技 術 ， 如Anylayer 、
mSAP、封裝載板、超高層板、高速傳輸及高
頻產品等各應用場域之需求。

2

營運展望 
1. 3Q26 需求強勁及漲價效應帶動營收獲利續創歷史新高：高速基材需求強勁、
帶動產品組合持續優化、及全品項漲價效應顯現，第三季營收續創歷史新高，
亦同時帶動毛利率進一步上揚。台光電第三季營收 609 億元、+28.8% QoQ、
+142.1% YoY， 毛利率 37.7%。 營業利益 187 億元、+46.8% QoQ、+274.5% YoY，
營業利益率 30.7%。歸屬母公司稅後淨利 145 億元，每股盈餘 40.47 元。期
末存貨總額提升至 271 億元，細項中原物料達 178 億元 、 製成品約 70 億元 、
在製品約 22 億元及商品約 7,774 萬元 ； 增備存貨主要著眼於高階原料市場吃
緊，藉由提前拉高原料水位以保障後續生產。 
2. 4Q26 需求仍然強勁將延續前幾季成長動能 ：第四季受惠產品結構與定價優勢 ，
將延續全產全銷強勁營運態勢，預估整體營收可望季增兩成。毛利率預期也因
定價優勢可進一步明顯提升。整體成長動能涵蓋 AI 伺服器、通用伺服器、高
階交換器、低軌衛星及記憶體模組等多重領域。高階基材整體價格持續調漲，
全產品線綜合漲價幅度預期落在 5 ~ 20% 之間 。基礎設施延續漲價效應及產品
組合優化將季增兩成，行動裝置因需求呈現持平，車用與工業則因產能優先挪
往高毛利產品，營收受排擠將季減約一成。 
3. 載板近期可望通過驗證並成為新一波成長動能：硬質銅箔基板涵蓋高密度互連
材料 (High Density Interconnect, HDI)、高速材料 (High Speed Digital, HSD) 與
載板 (Substrate) 三大範疇，雖然日韓對手高階 HDI 技術與台光電相仿，但擴
產停滯且侷限本土生產；台光電憑藉靈活量產規模與成本優勢，競爭力顯著勝
出。為追求極速傳輸與超低延遲，AI 系統架構加速走向模組化設計。單板晶片
密度倍增直接帶來線寬與線距極限微縮、高層數壓合等多重嚴苛的高階製程考
驗。台光電整合 HDI、高速與載板技術，推動「HDI 載板化」 ，確保板材歷經
多次高溫壓合仍具備載板級穩定性與可靠度。 
4. 產品組合持續往高階低損耗優化，新產能長約綁定將全產全銷：高階超低損耗
材料滲透率維持上升格局，M7+ 營收占比已從去年 50 ~ 55% 提升至 65 ~ 70%，
隨著 M9 下半年步入量產 ， 明年出貨規模放大 ， 佔比將進一步墊高至 70 ~ 75%。
產能也將自目前 585 萬張擴充至 2030 年 1,525 萬張。面對高階銅箔基板供
需日益緊俏且合規供應商有限下，終端客戶近期主動要求簽署長期供貨協議。 
5. 產品組合優化報價毛利率攀升，獲利將再創歷史新高：高階銅箔基板產能供不
應求，帶動產品組合持續大幅優化，推升報價和毛利率，今明年營運獲利將續
創歷史新高。 合庫預估 2026 年營收 2,086 億元、+121.3% YoY， 毛利率 35.3%。
營業利益 591 億元、+209.4% YoY，營業利益率 28.3%。歸屬母公司稅後淨利 
457 億元，每股盈餘 127.17 元。 
6. ESG (台光電在 2026 年上市公司治理評鑑結果為 6 ~ 20%)：台光電子針對各

3

項製造、服務與活動所衍生之空氣污染問題能加以管制及掌控。除了基本遵行
廢棄物清理法、水污染防治法、空氣污染防制法等相關環保法令外，每年度也
針對廠區環境保護投入相當金額，執行污染防治以維護環境品質。因應國際減
碳趨勢，以天然氣作為高碳過渡到低碳之能源。

達到高標。

營 運 分 析

聯發科為全球前五大 IC設計公司 
聯發科是台灣最大 IC設計公司，也是全球前五大無廠半導體(fabless)公司，
成立於 1997/05，並於 2001/07 掛牌上市。在無線通訊與數位媒體領域布局
多年。26Q3 三大產品線分為 Mobile Phone 37%、Smart Edge Platform 
58%、Power IC 5%，Mobile Phone產品以手機應用程式處理器(Application 
Processor)為主；Smart Edge Platform產品包括路由器、寬頻網路、藍芽耳
機等各種有線無線晶片、ARM架構運算單晶片、智慧電視、數位機上盒，以
及客製化晶片服務。

貢獻。Power IC 營收 QoQ+1%，YoY+18%，年增來自資料中心相關業務。

圖四、26Q4 AI ASIC放量 
聯發科各季營收及獲利走勢預估 (百萬元)

26Q4 ASIC上修，2027年 ASIC營收達到預估高標 
聯發科上修 26Q4資料中心 ASIC營收預估到 25億美元(前次預估 20億美元
以上)，主要因為客戶需求強勁，且爭取到供應鏈額外產能如ABF。雖然資料
中心 ASIC 的毛利率略低於公司平均，但因為供應鏈成本上升，漲價反應，
且資料中心業務的營業費用率低，營益率高於公司平均，26Q4 起加分效益
顯現。 
先前預估 2027 年可服務市場規模(SAM) 800 億美元，市佔目標 15~20%，
本次預估市佔目標達到高標。下一代 2nm ASIC專案預計 2028 年量產。 
第 2家雲端資料中心專案 design kickoff，預計 2028 年貢獻營收。公司表示
非中國客戶。 
技術佈局方面，已提前投資 Ayar Labs 佈局光通訊、CPO相關研發。448G 
SerDes已 tape out。 
26Q3 完成 39 億美元海外 CB 發行，NVIDIA、Alphabet 皆參與認購，有助
於未來 AI生態系合作。

### Page 3

Mediatek (2454.TW)
30 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Structural OPM upward trend — Management expects 4Q26 to mark the 
beginning of a structural improvement in OPM, supported by rapidly scaling data 
center ASIC revenue and operating leverage. Management indicated that the OPM 
could exceed 20% in 2027. Meanwhile, selective pricing adjustments across 
traditional businesses should help offset rising supply chain costs. Beyond data 
centers, Smart Edge should benefit from new AI PCs, AI tablets, automotive 
platforms and networking applications, including continued collaboration with 
NVIDIA on next-generation AI computing products. While smartphone demand 
remains relatively subdued, management expects the expanding AI business to 
increasingly dominate MediaTek’s overall growth and earnings trajectory.
Second-Generation ASIC on Track for 1Q28 
Production, with a Second Customer 
Management expressed increasing confidence in achieving the upper end of its 15–
20% market share target for the estimated US$80bn data center AI accelerator 
ASIC market in 2027, implying approximately US$16bn in potential revenue. This 
represents a substantial increase from the revised US$2.5bn target for 2026, 
supported by strong demand visibility and continued supply chain expansion. 
Management emphasized that securing adequate foundry, packaging and other 
critical component capacity remains essential to meeting customer requirements. 
Importantly, the revenue opportunity reflects MediaT ek’s increasing participation in 
the value chain, encompassing advanced process technologies, packaging, high-
speed interconnects and custom silicon integration. Despite recent market 
concerns over AI infrastructure financing and CSP capital expenditure 
sustainability, management remains confident that AI compute demand will 
continue growing over the next several years, particularly for customized 
accelerators offering superior performance per watt and total cost of ownership.

Management reiterated that its second AI accelerator project remains on schedule 
for high-volume production in 1Q28, despite market concerns regarding design 
complexity, advanced packaging yields and potential delays. The new accelerator is 
expected to deliver multiple times the performance per watt of the first-generation 
product, significantly improving customer TCO and strengthening Media T ek’s 
competitive positioning . Management acknowledged manufacturing challenges 
associated with its U.S. IDM packaging partner but highlighted progress in 
identifying key yield constraints and implementing corrective measures, expressing 
high confidence in further yield improvements. Importantly, Media T ek also 
confirmed a separate design win with a second, non-Chinese data center

Mediatek (2454.TW)

4
customer, with initial revenue expected in 2028. The company is already
collaborating with its existing customer on subsequent-generation products,
although specifications are still being finalized. Management expects another year
of strong revenue growth in 2028 but declined to provide specific revenue guidance
or customer contribution estimates.
Expanding competitive advantages through advanced node

design/packaging, SerDes and NVIDIA NVLink Fusion
MediaT ek continues investing in advanced technology capabilities, including 448G
SerDes, advanced packaging, HBM integration and co-packaged optics (CPO), to
establish a competitive advantage in increasingly complex AI infrastructure
designs. Management emphasized that its differentiation comes not from
individual technologies but from integrating them into high-performance, power-
efficient and commercially viable solutions. More importantly, its partnership with

NVIDIA through NVLink Fusion enables customers to integrate custom XPUs into
NVIDIA’s rack-scale ecosystem, while its support for UALink and Ethernet-based
architectures provides greater deployment flexibility. MediaT ek is expanding
beyond individual silicon components toward subsystems and rack-level
solutions, shortening customers’ development cycles and accelerating time to
revenue. Management indicated encouraging engagement with multiple potential

customers and expects broader adoption opportunities. The 448G SerDes
development is progressing well and is expected to support both existing
accelerator programs and future customers, with management not anticipating it
to become a critical schedule bottleneck.
Structural OPM expansion trend; Reiterate Buy
Management expects 4Q26 to mark the beginning of a structural improvement in
operating profitability, supported by rapidly scaling data center ASIC revenue and

operating leverage. Although ASIC gross margins are expected to remain below
MediaT ek’s traditional corporate average, the significantly larger revenue
contribution and more favorable operating expense leverage should drive
meaningful operating margin expansion. Management indicated that the operating
margin could exceed 20% in 2027, potentially somewhat higher, compared with its
historical mid-teen level. Meanwhile, selective pricing adjustments across

traditional businesses should help offset rising supply chain costs, although these
increases are primarily intended to preserve profitability rather than expand gross
margins. Beyond data centers, Smart Edge should benefit from new AI PCs, AI
tablets, automotive platforms and networking applications, including continued
collaboration with NVIDIA on next-generation AI computing products. While
smartphone demand remains relatively subdued, management expects the

expanding AI business to increasingly dominate MediaT ek’s overall growth and
earnings trajectory.
Given MediaT ek’s better operating leverage, we lift our earnings projection for
2027 /2028 5%/5%, mainly reflecting better OPM. We maintain our Buy rating with
an unchanged TP of NT$6,800, implying 29x our 2027 /28 EPS estimates.

### Page 9

Mediatek (2454.TW)
30 October 2026 Citi Research
9
Mediatek
Company description
Mediatek (MTK) is a fabless IC company that designs SoC for smartphone,
smart TV, and digital media devices. It also develops chips for connectivity,
networking, smart home gadgets, and automotive sectors. It was founded in
1997, is listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange, and is headquartered in Taiwan.
It has sales and research subsidiaries in mainland China, Singapore, India,

the US, Japan, Korea, Denmark, and England. It primarily sells its products in
China and other emerging-market countries.
Investment strategy
We rate Mediatek shares as Buy. We expect Mediatek to ride on 5G migration
in emerging markets and market share gain of its high-end Dimensity series in
China market as well. In particular, we see strong growth potential from its AI
ASIC, NTN-satellite business in the long term. We believe solid fundamentals

and strong operating leverage will support Mediatek’s margins outlook.
Valuation
Our NT$6,800 target price (rounded) for Mediatek is based on 29x P/E to
2027 /2028 EPS, which is 1-std above its five-year average forward P/E given
the promising outlook from its AI ASIC business starting from 2H26. We
believe our target PE is also justified by Mediatek's global and regional peers'
trading at an average of c32x PER. AI ASIC development with CSPs should

provide more upside from 2027 onwards. We also see Mediatek as well
prepared for 6G and 6G+NTN Satellite communication. Despite lukewarm
smartphone overall deamand, we expect Mediatek to benefit from 5G
migration in emerging markets and its Dimensity series should also gain
shares in flagship models. Non-smartphone business is also seeing
stabilizing TV and Io T demand with Mediatek outgrowing the overall market
on its better technology in WiFi 7 and connectivity.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth from AI ASIC business; 2)
more severe competition in the smartphone segment; 3) slower overall
demand related to macroeconomic risks, especially for smartphone demand;
and 4) further price hikes from foundries.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment
rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered

stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating
and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also

suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price
chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in

exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the
expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility

or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:
Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day

period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be
different from and does not affect a stock’s fundamental equity rating, which reflects a longer-term total absolute return

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
What's new? Earlier today, a fire broke out at Panasonic's (covered by Kazuo
Yoshikawa) CCL factory in Hsinchu, Taiwan. The fire was extinguished before noon
(see Reuters' report), but Panasonic has yet to issue an official statement in
response to the incident.
Our view: Our checks indicate that this plant has monthly capacity of ~200K sheets

and primarily produces M6/7 and some lower-end BT substrate CCLs. Panasonic also
operates CCL sites in Fukushima, Japan; Guangzhou and Suzhou, China; and
Ayutthaya, Thailand, which remains under construction. Most high-end M7/8 CCL is
produced in Fukushima, while the China sites supply much of its mainstream CCL.
Our supply chain checks suggest Panasonic is reallocating capacity toward high-end
CCL for AI applications – particularly a key US hyperscaler’s in-house ASICs, as high-

end CCL supply remains tight. Although the Hsinchu plant is immaterial to group
capacity, the fire could accelerate this shift, potentially driving near-term customer
order overflow to Taiwanese CCL competitors.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
A fire at Panasonic's CCL factory in Hsinchu may push near-term orderflow to
Taiwanese competitors.
Within our coverage, we see EMC as most likely to receive orders, followed by
TUC and Shengyi Tech (EW).
EMC is also a high-end CCL supplier to Panasonic’s key ASIC customer.
Valuations are attractive for EMC and TUC, and we remain OW on both.

November 4, 2026 04:01 PM GMT

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

法 說 前 瞻              產業：電子零組件 
群聯(8299)法說會前財報預估 
受惠 AI 帶動儲存需求，4Q26 營運動能延續

| 財報訊息   
群聯即將於 11/6(五)舉行法說會， 3Q26營收季增 26.79%至860.7億
元， 優於預期 ，市場普遍預估毛利率介於56~65%，EPS約124.22元，
關注公司對於2027年NAND市況、產業供需看法以及AI生態系佈局
與應用。 
| 營運現況  
3Q26營收季增26.79%，優於預期：3Q26營收季增26.79%至860.7億
元，優於預期，主要反映AI基礎建設、AI推論及企業級儲存需求快
速成長，2Q26公司AI Ecosystem modules 占比38%，然而9月公司AI 
Ecosystem modules 營收貢獻度已超過整體營收50%，考量在NAND
成本推升下，推估毛利率64.5%，稅後EPS 134.52元。 
| 營運展望  
預估4Q26營收季增 11.92%：預估 4Q26營收季增 11.92%至963.3億
元，動能來自持續受惠AI帶動儲存、運算、網路及相關基礎建設需
求，9月公司AI Ecosystem modules包括 eSSD 、aiDAPTIV solution、
AI PC、AI Networking、AI Server及Boot Drive開機碟等AI相關應用，
營收貢獻度已超過整體營收50%，反映公司Phison 3.0策略轉型，優
化產品結構 ， 目前公司近9成在於高附加價值市場應用 ， 跳脫以往傳
統零售業務景氣波動影響。先前法說公司提及目前 NAND供應短缺
仍是最大限制，預期2H26價格仍維持漲勢，考量隨著購入NAND成
本墊高下，推估毛利率62.4%，稅後EPS 142.08元。展望2027年，隨
著AI推理從文本、圖像、影片走向AI Agent產生TB級資料量，將推
升高容量、高效能儲存需求，將支撐公司後續營運動能。

3Q26 Review: 4 분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up  
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 600,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 292,500원(10/29)

 동사의 3분기 실적은 매출액 45.1조원(-3.4%yoy), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%yoy, OPM 5.9%), 지배이익 1.7조원(-24.6%yoy)을 기록하며 시장 
컨센서스를 상회. 도매 판매 감소, 원가 부담 증가 영향 있었으나 판매보증
충당금 축소와 원가 절감 활동을 통해 수익성 방어 
 실적 증감 요인: 영업이익 증감 요인은 물량 -1.11조원, 믹스/인센티브 -
0.57조원, 금융 -0.11조원, 환율 +1.28조원, 기타 +0.64조원 등. 원자재 
가격 상승으로 5천억원의 비용 증가 요인 있었으나 위기 대응 원가 절감 
활동으로 대부분 상쇄할 수 있었음. 전분기 대비 기말환율 급락함에 따라 
판매보증충당금 지난 해 1.26조원에서 340억원으로 약 1.23조원 감소. 판
보충 효과는 일회성에 그치겠지만 원가 절감 효과 컸던 점 긍정적  
 금융 부문: 금융부문 매출액은 전년비 2.8% 증가했지만 영업이익은 26% 
급감. 장기 금리 상승에 따른 이자 비용 증가와 기업금융 충당금 적립, 중고
차 관련 비용 확대가 수익성 악화의 주요 원인. 중고차 관련 비용은 EV 리
스 반납 차량의 매각 손실 확대에 기인한 것 
 4분기 전망: 동사는 국내 공장 정상화와 아반떼, 투싼, GV90, GV80 HEV 
등 주요 신차 효과를 통해 4분기 생산 확대와 주요 수출 시장 공급 회복이 
가능할 것으로 전망하였음. 원가 부담은 지속되나 물량 감소에 따른 영향에
서는 벗어날 수 있을 것. 원자재 가격 상승에 대응해 원가절감 프로그램 확
대와 신차 출시를 통한 인센티브 정상화를 통해 수익성 개선 지속할 계획 
 연간 가이던스 하향 조정: 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 기존 416만대에서 
400만대로 하향 조정. 매출 성장률은 +1~2%, OPM 가이던스는 기존 
6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정. 자동차 부문 투자 계획은 17.8조
원에서 16.0조원으로 축소. 핵심 R&D 투자는 유지하되 Capex 집행 시기 
및 우선 순위 조정할 계획 
 4분기 실적 반등 전망: 4분기 주요 신차(투싼, 아반떼, GV80 HEV, GV90) 
출시 본격화로 3분기까지의 부진을 만회할 수 있을 것으로 기대. 원가 부담 
은 비용 절감 활동을 통해 충분히 만회가 가능한 것으로 3분기 실적을 통
해 확인됨. 본격적인 주가 반등을 위해서는 판매 회복 이상의 주가 트리거 
필요할 것으로 보이나, 4분기 반전의 실마리가 나타나고 있다는 점에 주목. 
동사에 대한 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 60만원을 유지함

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%)

주가 추이

3Q26 매 출액 과 영 업 이익 은 비현 금 성 비용 항목 개 선 으로 시장 기대치
를 상 회 했 으나, 차량 판매 감소와 인 플 레 이션 등으로 연간 가이 던 스 는 
하 향 되 었 습니 다. 올해 생산 차질 에 따라 이 연된 신차 판 매를 ’27년에 
확대하며 매출/이익 회복을 가시 화 하 는 부분 에 주목 필 요 합니다. 
매출 성장은 금융부문이, 영업이익은 자동차부문이 기여 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 45.1조원(-3.5% 이하 Y oY), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%)을 기록하며 시장 기대치를 상회했다. 파업으로 인한 생산 차질
로 3Q 판매량 92.1만대(-11.3%)에 그치며 자동차부문 매출은 큰 폭으
로 감소(33조원, -10.0%)했으나, 금융부문 매출이 취급 자산 증가로 큰 
폭으로 성장(+27.8%)했다. 반면, 영업이익은 금리 상승에 따른 금융부문 
영업비용 확대 영향과 기타부문 실적 부진 발생과 달리 자동차부문은 전
년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소와 판매보증 충당부채 전입
액 감소 등 우호적 환율효과로 큰 폭의 증가세(+24.8%) 기록했다. 
’26년 가이던스 하향, 구조적 반등 요인은 아직 
3Q 실적 발표에서 주목할 부분은 가이던스 하향과 현금흐름이다. 연간 
판매목표를 연초 415.8만대에서 400만대로 하향했고 영업이익률 목표
는 6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 0.8%p 하향했으며, ’26년 투자계획은 
17.8조원에서 16조원으로 낮췄다. 특히, 투자계획 하향은 차량 판매 매
출 감소 대비 인센티브, 원자재 가격 상승 등으로 현금흐름이 악화된 
부분에 기인한 것으로 판단된다. 실적발표에서 전사적 원가 최적화 활
동 추진을 강조했으나, 이는 좀 더 장기적 측면에서 효과가 가시화될 
것으로 판단되며, 인플레이션 및 원화절상 상황 고려 시 구조적 반등은 
단기적으로 쉽지 않을 수 있다. 
절치부심의 2027년, 목표주가 600.000원 유지 
그러나 매크로 상황을 제 외 하 면  ’26년 하반기 생산 차질에 따른 신차 
판매효과가 ’27년 으 로 이연된 것 이 라 는 점에 주목 필요하다.   ‘북미 HEV, 유럽 
BEV’ 전략 하에서 엘란트라, 투싼 풀세인지, GV80, G80 등 고수익 HEV 차종 
중심 미국 수요 대응을 통해 Mix 개선하는 모습이 확인되고, 생산이 본 격 화 된 
아이오닉3 유럽 판매 확대 시 중국 로 컬 업 체 들 의 공세를 방 어 하 는 모 습 을 
확인하는 것이 가 능 할 것으로 판 단 한 다. 노 후 차 종 중심 운 영 에 따른 판매 둔화 
이외에 아직 근 원 적 판매 경쟁력 저하로는 판단하지 않는다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 30일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 6

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

1.5% in Q3 and 4.6%yr.

with average savings buffers increasing 
to 22.2 months of mortgage repayments.

remain uneven but spending patterns 
appear to be converging across 
consumers with low, medium and high 
buffers. Much of this appears to relate 
to a reduction in fuel consumption.

Westpac–DataX Consumer Panel
The Westpac–DataX Consumer Panel is a large 
dataset that gives a timely and detailed picture 
of Australian consumer finances and behaviour. 
Developed by DataX, Westpac’s data analytics 
team, the dataset links transaction activity with 
balance sheet information to give a complete view 
of income, spending, saving and borrowing flows. 
All data is de-identified and aggregated to 
ensure privacy. The resulting sample of over 
one million customers gives an accurate 
representation of trends across the wider 
Australian consumer and is perfectly suited 
to quickly tracing responses to events like 
interest rate moves and energy price shocks.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Heatmap of monthly spending categories
The Westpac DataX Consumer Panel  shows average
consumer spending remained resilient in Q3, rising 1.5%
despite a further lift in fuel costs. Fuel contributed 0.3ppts
to quarterly growth, but discretionary spending did more

of the heavy lifting, contributing 0.9ppts as recreation
and travel-related spending recovered. Households also
continued to add to their financial buffers: savings up 1.3%
in the quarter and average buffers across households with
a mortgage increasing to 22.2 months of repayments.
Beneath the aggregate picture, lower-buffer households
remain more exposed to higher fuel costs and mortgage
repayments, but their discretionary spending share is
looking increasingly similar to other households.

Spending holds up despite higher fuel costs
Average household spending increased 1.5% in Q3, with much
of the strength again driven by transport-related spending.
Fuel contributed 0.3ppts to quarterly growth, while transport
excluding fuel contributed a further 0.4ppts. Excluding the
direct effects of the price-driven lift in fuel, average spend
was up 1.2%qtr. Importantly, stronger fuel spending did not
come at the expense of discretionary purchases. Discretionary

categories contributed 0.9ppts to quarterly spending growth,
compared with 0.5ppts from non-fuel essentials.
Solid rise in non-fuel spend and financial buffers
Discretionary spending dominates
Recreation and culture contributed 0.3ppts to growth, reflecting
stronger spending on sporting services, while restaurants and
hotels added a further 0.2ppts. Transport spending was also
boosted by a rebound in airline expenditure, which more than
unwound the weakness recorded in Q2 when refunds relating

to cancellations due to conflict in the Middle East weighed on
the category. Discretionary spending rose 0.9%qtr, following a
flat result and a 0.1% rise in the previous two quarters.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Savings growth by mortgage status
Households continue to rebuild savings
Savings increased 1.3% in Q3, with the strongest gains again 
recorded among younger households. Savings balances for 
18–34 year olds increased 2.0%qtr, compared with 0.7% 
for those aged 55 and over. Mortgagors also continued to 
accumulate savings more rapidly, recording a 2.8% increase 
compared with 0.7% among non-mortgagors. Savings growth 
among mortgagors has now outpaced non-mortgagors for 
three consecutive years.
Stronger savings have continued to lift mortgage buffers. 
Average buffers (savings divided by the sum of essential 
expenses and minimum mortgage repayments), increased to 
22.2 months of repayments in September. Looking within the 
unit-record sample, median mortgage buffers jumped from 
6.3 months in August to 6.8 months, its highest level since the 
series began in 2019.

Median mortgage buffers
Fuel spending a clear drag on consumers
Fuel spending as a share of total spending
Spending outcomes continued to vary across age groups. 
The squeeze remains most apparent among younger 
households. Fuel spending rose 5.1%qtr for 18–34 year olds, 
against a 1.5%qtr increase in non-fuel spending. By contrast, 
non-fuel spending increased 2.1%qtr among households aged 
55 and over. 
The gap between mortgagors and non-mortgagors was 
more modest. Spending rose 1.6%qtr among mortgagors 
and 1.9%qtr among those without a mortgage, suggesting 
mortgage status alone masks considerable variation in 
household financial positions – a divide that becomes clearer 
when households are grouped by the size of their mortgage 
buffers (see discussion further on in this note). Note that ‘non 
mortgagors’ covers a diverse range of consumers including 
those that are renting and those that own their homes without 
a mortgage.

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Median litres of fuel consumed
More importantly, their consumption appears to be less 
responsive to price movements. During periods of cheaper 
fuel, high-buffer households show a noticeably larger lift in 
litres purchased, consistent with stockpiling when prices are 
favourable. Low-buffer households show much less of this 
response, while their fuel volumes have also adjusted less 
during the recent increase in fuel costs.
The spending data show the consequence. Fuel expenditure 
has risen sharply across all three groups recently, but remains 
highest among low-buffer households, where it also absorbs 
a larger share of total spending. Food spending has also 
declined since 2023. This may partly reflect households 
economising within the category, including switching towards 
cheaper or home-brand products, although further work is 
needed to test this.
Taken together, the households with the smallest savings 
cushions face higher mortgage repayments, spend more 
on fuel and appear to have less scope to adjust their fuel 
consumption when prices change. 
Discretionary spend as a share of total
This suggests that in aggregate, household balance sheets 
are looking healthy. The picture is a little at odds with the very 
weak reads on our Westpac-Melbourne Index Consumer 
Sentiment Index which suggests finances are coming under 
much more pressure. The fact that both average spending and 
saving both rose suggests incomes over the quarter were not 
overly stretched and that more of the weakness in sentiment 
may relate to concerns about the outlook (something that may 
also be encouraging consumers to build buffers now, while they 
still can).
Pressures intense for low-buffer households
Aggregate buffer measures mask sizeable differences across 
mortgagors. We can get a sense of these variations by 
combining mortgagors into three broad groups according to 
buffer size, i.e. low, middle and high buffer groups. This shows 
that households with the smallest buffers are also more 
exposed to both interest rates and fuel costs.

The buffer differences are relatively stark, the median savings 
of the low-buffer group just $2k compared to $15k for the 
middle-buffer group and $95k for the high buffer group. 
Low buffer households face median minimum mortgage 
repayments of around $2,600 a month, compared with $2,400 
for the middle buffer group and $2,200 for the highest-buffer 
group. 
Low buffer households also spend more on fuel, at around 
$170 a month compared with $160 for the middle buffer 
group and $150 for the high buffer group.

We can use average pump prices to estimate what this 
expenditure implies for fuel volumes. Low-buffer households 
consume around 70 litres a month, middle-buffer households 
67 litres a month and the high-buffer group around 62 litres. 
This compares to an average of 81, 80 and 70 respectively 
through 2024-25. 
Median fuel spend by customer

### Page 5

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Yet despite this greater exposure, the gap in broader spending 
behaviour has narrowed. High-buffer households continue 
to spend more on discretionary categories, averaging around 
$1,940 a month compared with $1,740 for low-buffer 
households. However, their discretionary share has been 
broadly stable since 2023, while the shares of low- and 
middle-buffer households have risen by close to a percentage 
point bringing them more in line with the high-buffer group.

Some of the convergence reflects a longer run down-trend in 
fuel spending that has been more pronounced amongst low 
and middle buffer households. This lowering in fuel spend lifts 
the share available for other categories.

Essential spending excluding fuel, meanwhile, has changed 
little across the three cohorts since 2023. The shift in 
spending patterns has largely occurred on the discretionary 
side.

The result is a somewhat mixed picture. Low-buffer 
households remain considerably more exposed to mortgage 
and fuel costs and have far smaller savings cushions. But their 
discretionary spending has nevertheless moved closer to that 
of better-buffered households, suggesting some recovery in 
spending flexibility despite these constraints. 
Conclusion
Aggregate household finances remain resilient: spending and 
savings both increased in Q3 and mortgage buffers continued 
to rebuild. Discretionary spending has also held up despite the 
sharp increase in fuel costs.

But aggregate resilience masks a much more uneven 
distribution of pressure. Low-buffer households 
simultaneously face higher mortgage repayments, greater fuel 
exposure and much smaller savings cushions. While these 
households have so far maintained discretionary spending, 
their capacity to absorb further pressure is clearly more 
limited.
About the Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel
Developed by DataX, Westpac’s data analytics service for 
institutional and business banking customers, the dataset links 
transaction activity with balance sheet information to give 
a complete view of income, spending, saving and borrowing 
flows. 
The resulting sample of over one million customers provides 
an accurate representation of trends across the wider 
Australian consumer. Monthly observations are available back 
to January 2019 with timely updates provided a few weeks 
after the end of each month. As such, it is perfectly suited to 
quickly tracing the impact and responses to events like interest 
rate moves and energy price shocks.

Document 317: Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower_

Subject: Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower?
Date:    1 hour ago
Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Could Midterms Give Wall Street
A Cold Shower?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 2 Nov 2026
View in browser

I. When Gridlock Isn't Good for Stocks
The US midterm elections will be over tomorrow night. According to
betting markets, Democrats have roughly a 95% probability of winning the
House and a 76% probability of taking the Senate (charts).
We suspect that stock investors have yet to fully discount the possibility of
a Democratic sweep of Congress. Some may remain skeptical that polls
confirming strong support for Democrats are accurate. They might reckon

### Page 2

that Democrats are more likely than Republicans to talk to pollsters over
the phone.
In any event, Wall Street traditionally welcomes political gridlock because
it reduces the likelihood that Congress will pass extremist legislation. But
this time could be different for two reasons.
First, deepening partisanship could turn gridlock into damaging political
paralysis at a time when the Bond Vigilantes are already uneasy about
Washington's unsustainable fiscal trajectory. A Democratic sweep could

complicate negotiations over government spending and the debt ceiling.
Prolonged shutdowns and fiscal standoffs could push Treasury yields
higher, weighing on equity valuations.

### Page 3

Second, gridlock's traditional ability to constrain policymaking may not
hold this time. President Donald Trump has relied heavily on executive
authority, with some of his most market-disruptive policies, including tariffs
and military action against Iran, pursued largely without congressional
approval. A Democratic-controlled Congress may therefore do little to
restrain him. Moreover, with the midterms behind him, Trump may feel
even less constrained politically and more willing to pursue controversial

policies through executive action. Hence, a blue wave could deliver a cold
shower for stocks, though we would view a significant selloff as a buying
opportunity given the remarkable strength of the economy and corporate
earnings.
One encouraging development ahead of the elections was today's reports
that the US and Iran are working on a peaceful diplomatic solution to their
war. Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian said he has granted his
government greater authority to pursue peace talks, while President

Trump and Vice President Vance also signaled progress. Hopes for a deal
sent the price of Brent crude oil down 7.6% to $104.77 per barrel (chart).
However, we have been here before with apparent diplomatic
breakthroughs. We still see a high risk of military re-escalation. The bid-
ask spread remains wide, with Tehran demanding sanctions relief, the
release of frozen assets, and an end to the US naval blockade of Iranian
ports, while Washington insists on nuclear concessions. Meanwhile, Iran's

leverage over the Strait of Hormuz has been weakening. According to
shipping data, for roughly half of September, Gulf oil exporters shipped
more crude than they were shipping before the war.

### Page 4

II. US Economy Continues To Roar
The US economy remains strong despite the volatility of the domestic
political and geopolitical scenes. Consider the following:
(1) Construction spending. Construction spending fell 1.9% m/m in
September to $2.19 trillion (saar), reversing most of August's upwardly
revised 2.0% increase. Nevertheless, spending remains just below last
year's record high (chart).
September's decline was driven by a 5.5% m/m drop in private residential

construction, attributable to a 13.0% plunge in home improvements
(chart). Elevated materials costs and higher mortgage rates continue to
weigh on the housing sector. Meanwhile, private nonresidential
construction rose 1.1% m/m, supported by continued investment in data
centers and power infrastructure.

### Page 5

The AI investment boom remains a powerful tailwind. Data center
construction spending surged 8.6% m/m and 90% y/y in September,
reaching a record $96.5 billion annual rate (chart).
(2) Manufacturing. The ISM M-PMI rose from 54.5 in September to 55.5
in October, marking its tenth consecutive month of expansion, the longest
streak since 2022. Production jumped to the highest reading since
November 2021, while new orders edged up to 55.5. Employment also

remained in expansionary territory (chart).

### Page 6

Inflationary pressures remain troublesome. The prices-paid index eased
from 77.9 to 75.2 in October but remained above 70 for the ninth
consecutive month. Regional Fed surveys also point to persistent price
pressures. Higher energy costs, tariffs, and elevated metals prices
continue to boost input costs (chart).
(3) Bank lending. The Fed's latest Senior Loan Officer Opinion Survey
(SLOOS) painted a mixed picture of credit demand. Banks reported
weaker demand for commercial and industrial (C&I) loans, mortgages,

and auto loans in Q4, while credit-card demand strengthened (chart).

### Page 7

Lending standards remained broadly unchanged for C&I loans but
tightened somewhat for commercial real estate loans (chart).
Encouragingly, actual bank lending tells a happier story. C&I loans surged
11.3% y/y through October 21, while total loans and leases increased
7.9% y/y (chart).

### Page 8

Credit growth is broad across bank sizes, with lending up 8.1% y/y at
large banks and 5.4% at small banks (chart).
Meanwhile, banks' allowances for loan and lease losses have remained
relatively stable at around $202 billion, despite the acceleration in lending
(chart).

### Page 9

clause.

### Page 1

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

India: Degrees of rupee defence

16 October 2026 
Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 31.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.4%. 
 Of the 299 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 81.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 13.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 12.2%. 
 69.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 66 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 66 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 19.0. 
 During the week of Nov. 2, 144 S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $778.4B 
from $760.7B). Three of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-20.5%) and materials (-7%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (33%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. 
Overall, share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 2.3% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-14.7B) and materials (-1.2B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (15.7B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 17.7B since the start of the quarter.

299 companies in the S&P 500 Index have reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these companies, 81.6% reported 
earnings above analyst expectations and 15.7% reported earnings below analyst expectations. In a typical quarter 
(since 1994), 67% of companies beat estimates and 20% miss estimates. Over the past four quarters, 82% of 
companies beat the estimates and 15% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 4.5% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
1994) average surprise factor of 4.4% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 7.7%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 31.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.4%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $778.4B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.4B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer 
Discretionary sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (146.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $63B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.6B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (566.5%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (143.8%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 39%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (68.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $260.2B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $154B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (137%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (71.5%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 28.2%. 
The Consumer Discretionary sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn 
$56.3B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $54.2B in 25Q3. 7 of the 18 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to 
see lower earnings than a year ago. The Casinos & Gaming (-44.1%) and Automotive Parts & Equipment (-38.8%) 
sub-industries have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate 
improves to 4.5%.

The estimated revenue growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 13.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth rate declines  to 12.2%. The S&P 
500 expects to see revenue of $4898.8B in 26Q3, compared to revenue of $4310.4B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 
constituents) in 25Q3.

11 of the 11 sectors anticipate revenue growth for the quarter. The Information Technology sector has the highest revenue growth rate for the 
quarter, while the Financials sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3.

The Information Technology sector has the highest revenue growth rate (40.7%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $772.3B in 26Q3, compared to 
revenue of $548.8B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher revenue than a year ago. The Semiconductors 
(88%) and Semiconductor Materials & Equipment (49.4%) sub-industries have the highest revenue growth in the sector. If these su b-industries are 
removed, the growth rate declines to 23.7%.

The Financials sector has the lowest revenue growth rate (5.1%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $576.7B in 26Q3, compared  to revenue of 
$548.4B in 25Q3. 3 of the 13 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower revenue than a year ago. The Diversified  Financial Services (-
40.9%) and Reinsurance (-18.1%) sub-industries have the lowest revenue growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are remove d, the growth rate 
improves to 6%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 147 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, 17 positive

surprises and 11 negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
Markets were generally buoyed overnight, with Anthropic’s
IPO filing and Meta’s US$25bn equity offering closing five
times oversubscribed adding momentum back into the
AI trade, while softer oil prices provided further support.

Attention now turns to a series of key economic developments,
with Australian CPI due today alongside policy decisions and
accompanying guidance from the RBNZ, BoC and FOMC.
• US equities moved higher, supported by AI-related
stocks. The S&P 500 gained 0.8%, while the NASDAQ
outperformed, rising 1.2%, and the Dow Jones was
unchanged. European markets were mixed, with the Euro
Stoxx 50 up 0.8%, while the FTSE 100 fell 0.8% ahead of
the UK Budget.
• Tech-heavy Asian markets were buoyed as recent headlines

helped ease concerns around AI financing. The KOSPI led
gains, rising 3.1%, while the Nikkei 225 also performed
strongly, up 1.7%. The Hang Seng diverged from regional
peers, falling 0.7%. Locally, the ASX 200 gained 0.5%.
• US Treasury yields were little changed, with the 2-year
yield rising 1bp and the 10-year yield unchanged. Futures
markets remain cautious about pricing further Fed tightening
in 2026, with the probability of a hike at today’s FOMC

meeting sitting at just 7%, taking the implied year-end fed
funds rate to 4.10%.
• Australian government bond yields were little changed
ahead of today’s CPI release, with the 3-year yield rising
1bp and the 10-year yield falling 1bp. Swap markets remain
cautious around additional RBA tightening in 2026, with the
probability of a November rate hike priced at 62%, taking
the implied year-end cash rate to 4.80%.
• Pre-FOMC positioning supported the US dollar, with

the DXY up 0.3%. The AUD outperformed other major
currencies, rising 0.2% against the greenback to 0.7028 and
also gaining across the major crosses. The euro and yen both
fell 0.3% against the USD, while the sterling declined 0.2%.
• Oil prices declined on hopes that diplomacy could avert a
renewed escalation in the US-Iran conflict. Brent fell 1.6%
to US$114/bbl, while WTI declined 1.9% to US$99.9/bbl.
Dated Brent traded at US$121/bbl. Gold rose 1.1% ahead

of today’s FOMC meeting, while positive risk sentiment
supported base metals, with copper up 1.0% and aluminium
gaining 1.5%. Iron ore fell 1.1%, remaining below US$90/t
amid persistent concerns around global demand.
International Data
US durable goods orders rose 1.8% in September after a 0.1%
gain in August. Growth for core orders (non defence, ex aircraft)
was more modest in September at 0.5% but follows a 1.6% rise
in August.
US S&P Cotality Case Shiller house prices rose a further 0.3%

in August, accelerating annual growth at the margin to 2.7%yr.
US new home sales fell 0.6% in September following a
downwardly revised 5.1% gain (prev 6.4%) in August. Sales are
currently near the average of the past 5 years.
US Conference Board consumer confidence jumped from 84.2
to 91.7 in October on an improvement in current conditions
and expectations. While still below average, this measure of
consumer confidence continues to outperform the University of

Michigan survey, held up by the robust strength of the labour
market. Responses to the Michigan measure instead look to be
focused more on cost of living.
The Richmond Fed manufacturing index was unchanged at -2
in October.
Local Data
Our New Zealand team published their 2026 General Election
preview, outlining the major parties’ key policies (see here).
